## [0133] Fleet worker version gate — stale workers claim nothing, loudly — 2026-07-09 (feat/fleet-worker-version-gate)
**What:** Closed `fleet-worker-version-gate-1` (the Oliver incident, worklog 0125: a stale Windows worker silently outraced the head and served pre-#84 extracts for days). Every process now derives a monotonic **code version** at import — new `app/services/code_version.py`: `git rev-list --count HEAD` (orderable on the linear squash-merge branch) + `git rev-parse --short HEAD`; `WORKER_CODE_VERSION=<int>` env override; **shallow clones are refused loudly** (their count is the truncated fetch depth — error names `git fetch --unshallow`), missing git → no version. Mig **0046** adds `budget_state.min_worker_version` (+ `stamped_by`/`stamped_at`); `main.lifespan` **auto-stamps raise-only** (`budget_repo.raise_version_floor`, SQL `WHERE floor IS NULL OR floor < :v`) — restarting any box on newer code fences every stale worker; a stale restart is a no-op. `worker._claim_one` refuses **before** `claim_next_job` when `code_version.is_stale(CODE_VERSION, floor)` — fail-closed (unknown version + floor = blocked), zero added queries (floor rides the existing per-claim `get_state` read), throttled ERROR ≤1/300s with grep token **`version gate: STALE`**. Visibility: heartbeat blob gains `code_version`/`git_sha`; `claimed_by` = `hostname:pid@sha` (durable post-hoc attribution); `GET /workers` gains per-row `capabilities` + top-level `version_floor`; FE worker cards show `v{n} @sha` + red **STALE** chip + `floor v{n}`; `PUT /workers/version-floor {"value": int|null}` = unconditional operator escape hatch (may LOWER/clear — the recovery for a head that stamped with unpushed local commits).
**Why:** `claimed_by` recorded host:pid but not code vintage; nothing stopped a stale worker from claiming. Hard gate + loud + visible beats advisory (locked with user 2026-07-09, all three recommended options).
**Files:** `app/services/code_version.py` (new), `alembic/versions/0046_worker_version_floor.py` (new), `app/models/budget_state.py`, `app/repositories/budget.py`, `app/services/worker.py`, `main.py`, `app/repositories/workers.py`, `app/api/v1/workers.py`, `web/src/lib/{types,api}.ts`, `web/src/components/fleet/worker-cards.tsx`, 6 new test files (+2 extended).
**Verified:** suite **1544 passed / 0 failed** (canonical, no RUN_DB_INTEGRATION); raise-only SQL predicate real-DB-proven on scratch `edu_scratch_vgate` (100→150 raises, 150→120 REFUSED, escape hatch lowers/clears); tsc + vite build clean. **Live acceptance ($0, scratch DB, both legs):** worker at env version 100 vs floor 999999 polled 12+ cycles → exactly ONE throttled `version gate: STALE` ERROR, claimed nothing, floor un-lowered; worker at 1000000 → startup log "version floor raised to 1000000", job claimed with `claimed_by=Macs-Mac-mini.local:5850@6bb3d51`, `GET /workers` showed floor + both workers' version/sha, `PUT {"value":null}` cleared (stamped_by=operator). Final whole-branch review (fable): Ready-to-merge-with-fixes; both Importants closed (this docs commit + inverted `setVersionFloor` comment fixed in 32d5672).
**Notes / DEPLOY NOTE:** (1) The gate only bites once a box runs post-merge code — **workers on pre-gate code are NOT enforced** (their `_claim_one` has no gate) but DO render STALE on the fleet page once a floor exists (missing blob keys → fail-closed FE render). Oliver specifically stays visible-not-fenced until 192.168.1.16 pulls once; the owed fleet pull (#84+#88+#90+#91+this) is the rollout — after it, every future missed pull idles loudly instead of silently serving stale output. (2) **Fleet pre-flight before the rollout restart:** on each host `git rev-parse --is-shallow-repository` must print `false`; if `true`, run `git fetch --unshallow` first (a shallow clone reads as versionless and idles once a floor exists). (3) Enforcement is claim-time-only — a worker mid-job when the floor rises finishes that in-flight job on old code (by design). (4) A manually lowered/cleared floor stays down until any post-gate process restarts — there is no background re-raise (`raise_version_floor`'s only caller is `main.lifespan`). (5) Mig 0046 must be applied to the head DB (`uv run alembic upgrade head`). Diagnostics: grep worker log for `version gate: STALE`; fleet page STALE chip. Deferred minors (final-review triage, all defer): unguarded `int(count)` at rev-list success; local `import time` in `_log_stale_gate` (plan-mandated); `stamped_by="operator"` constant loses the acting user; `api.setVersionFloor` FE client currently uncalled (curl-parity). → [[session-state-2026-07-07-sse-multipod-bus]], worklog 0125.

## [0134] Codex-CLI model list refreshed to live backend models — 2026-07-13 (Nggaev-v2 direct)
**What:** `MODEL_MANIFEST["codex"]` was stale — 4 of its 5 entries (`gpt-5.2`, `gpt-5`, `gpt-5-mini`, `gpt-5-nano`) no longer exist on the codex backend; only `gpt-5.5` survived. Replaced with the 7 currently-selectable models read from the codex CLI's own authoritative list (`~/.codex/models_cache.json`, `visibility="list"`, client 0.144.0, fetched 2026-07-13): `gpt-5.5` (kept first = the CLI's `config.toml` default, so `default_model("codex")` stays coherent with what codex runs on model=None), `gpt-5.6-sol` (latest frontier agentic), `gpt-5.6-terra` (balanced), `gpt-5.6-luna` (fast/affordable), `gpt-5.4` (strong everyday), `gpt-5.4-mini` (small/cost-efficient), `gpt-5.3-codex-spark` (ultra-fast). Dropped the hidden `codex-auto-review` (visibility=`hide`, internal approval-review model, not a content model). Synced `model_tiers._MODEL_TIER` (completeness test requires every manifest model to carry a tier): T1 sol+5.5, T2 terra+5.4, T3 luna, T4 5.4-mini+codex-spark; removed the 4 dead codex tiers. Updated `test_model_tiers.py` codex example from the removed `gpt-5-nano` to `gpt-5.4-mini`.
**Why:** User wants **codex over the CLI** (`transport=cli`, subscription $0-marginal — explicitly NOT the api path; codex api stays deferred / `fleet-api-5`). The manifest gates `POST /generate`, so a stale entry would let a user create a codex job on a model the backend rejects → hard failure at spawn. codex is CLI-only (not in `API_PROVIDERS`) and the CLI path was never deleted (it's the schema default + fallback), so no code beyond the model list was needed to make codex generatable.
**Files:** `app/services/agent_models.py`, `app/services/model_tiers.py`, `tests/services/test_model_tiers.py`.
**Verified:** Full suite **1544 passed / 214 skipped** (canonical, no RUN_DB_INTEGRATION). Runtime check: `is_valid("codex","gpt-5.6-sol")=True`, removed models `gpt-5`/`gpt-5-nano` → `False`, `default_model("codex")="gpt-5.5"`, `api_supported("codex")=False`. **Live acceptance ($0, subscription):** real `codex exec --json --model gpt-5.6-sol` returned `OK` with a `turn.completed` usage event (input 12,980 / cached 9,984 / output 5) — a newly-added model resolves end-to-end on the backend, not just in the cache file.
**Notes:** No FE mirror to update — the frontend fetches the list dynamically from `/api/v1/agent/models` (`web/src/lib/api.ts:230`), not a hardcoded copy. No pricing entries (CLI rows bill $0 — subscription; codex would be invisible in the per-transport $ rollups, same as the retired cli path). `_PROVIDER_DEFAULT_MODEL["codex"]` stays `None` (agent.py no-leak invariant untouched → `_resolve_model("codex",None) is None`; the codex CLI supplies its own default). No migration. **To actually RUN codex jobs the fleet must (a) have the `codex` CLI installed + logged in on each worker and (b) launch with `provider=codex, transport=cli`** — off the api production path the campaign uses. Standing decision unchanged: real generation runs `transport=api` on gemini/claude; codex-CLI is an opt-in per-job choice, not the default lane.

## [0135] Fleet launcher: content model was unreachable on transport=cli — 2026-07-14 (Nggaev-v2 direct)
**What:** On `transport=cli` the fleet launcher made the **content model** impossible to set — the `/settings` `launch_defaults.content_model` was silently discarded and the CLI fell back to its own config default. Three independent gates in `launcher.tsx`, all cli-only: (1) the reconcile effect ran `setModel(null)` on cli, wiping the model the seeding effect had just loaded from `ld.content_model`; (2) the model `<Select>` was rendered only when `transport === "api"` — there was no picker at all on cli; (3) the launch payload spread `...(transport === "api" ? { model } : {})`, so the field wasn't even sent. Extracted the rule into a pure `web/src/lib/launch-model.ts` (`resolveLaunchModel` + `PROVIDER_DEFAULT` sentinel + `toSelectValue`/`fromSelectValue`), wired all three sites to it: cli now KEEPS a valid explicit pick (it becomes `--model X`) and drops only a model belonging to a different provider (→ `null` = provider default); the picker renders on both transports, with an extra **"Provider default"** item on cli; the payload always sends `model`.
**Why:** User set every role to codex in `/settings` (content `gpt-5.3-codex-spark`, extract `gpt-5.4-mini`, judge `gpt-5.5`, solver `gpt-5.4`) and launched 2 history-g7 jobs. Extract/judge/solver honored their models; **content did not** — it ran `<provider-default>` = codex's own `config.toml` default `gpt-5.5`. Knock-on: the generator then resolved to `codex/gpt-5.5`, which **self-matched the `codex/gpt-5.5` judge**, so `model_tiers.resolve_judge`'s no-self guard hard-swapped the judge to `claude/claude-opus-4-7` — the "why is there claude?" symptom. Violated the standing rule that a per-job model pick must be honored on every code path.
**Files:** `web/src/lib/launch-model.ts` (new), `web/src/lib/launch-model.test.ts` (new), `web/src/components/fleet/launcher.tsx`.
**Verified:** RED-proven (`npx tsx src/lib/launch-model.test.ts` fails ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND before the module exists) → GREEN; 4 sibling FE pure tests still pass; `tsc -p tsconfig.app.json --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` ✓ 2380 modules. Backend suite **1544 passed / 214 skipped** (untouched). **Live acceptance ($0, codex CLI subscription):** `build_argv` emits `--model gpt-5.3-codex-spark`; `_resolve_model("codex","gpt-5.3-codex-spark")` keeps it while `("codex",None)` stays `None`; and a REAL `agent.run_phase(provider="codex", model="gpt-5.3-codex-spark")` spawned and returned `OK` (16,016 prompt / 157 output tokens).
**Notes:** **FE-only — the backend was already correct** and is deliberately untouched: `is_valid(codex, None)=True` (cli provider-default stays legal) and `is_valid(codex,"gpt-5.3-codex-spark")=True`, so it validates and stamps whatever arrives; `build_argv` already adds `--model X` whenever the model is truthy. **Zero API-transport impact** — `validate_transport` rejects `transport=api` + `model=None` with a 400, so api always carried a concrete model and its branch is unchanged. Considered and REJECTED a backend `model = body.model or ld.content_model` fallback: (a) `resolve_role_selection` with an explicit provider returns `default_model(provider)` (manifest-first = `gpt-5.5`), NOT `ld.content_model`, so reusing it wouldn't deliver the user's pick anyway; (b) a raw `or` fallback would let an `ld.content_model` belonging to a *different* provider (e.g. a codex model on a gemini launch) bypass `is_valid`, which runs on `body.model` *before* `ld` is loaded — it would need a resolve-then-validate reorder for no gain once the FE sends the model. `section.tsx` (single-job `/generate`) never had the bug — it renders the picker unconditionally and always sends `model`. Side effect: with content on `gpt-5.3-codex-spark` the judge no longer self-matches `codex/gpt-5.5`, so the claude self-grade swap stops firing. `web/dist` is gitignored — rebuilt locally; fleet workers need no rebuild (FE is served from the head).

## [0136] SA-key panel labels keys by owner Gmail, not project id — 2026-07-14 (Nggaev-v2 direct)
**What:** The Fleet SA-keys panel (`sa-keys-panel.tsx`) headlined each key by `project_id` (an opaque `project-43a667fb…` string), which is meaningless for telling whose key is whose. Switched the three display sites to show the **owner Gmail** instead: the key-pool row headline, the host-assignment cell, and the "Assign key…" dropdown options. New pure helper `web/src/lib/sa-key-label.ts` `keyLabel(originalFilename, projectId)` — strips a trailing `.json` (case-insensitive) and returns the result IF it's an email (contains `@`), else falls back to `project_id` (so the default `key.json` upload name doesn't render a useless "key"). project_id + client_email demoted to the small secondary line. The assignment cell has no `original_filename` in its payload, so it resolves the key from the in-scope `keys` pool by `key_id` and labels that (fallback: `a.project_id`).
**Why:** User distributes SA keys named `<gmail>.json` (e.g. `abdscorpion00@gmail.com.json`) and wanted keys identified by the owning Google account. The Gmail is NOT in the key JSON (only the robot `client_email` `veo-gemini-sa@<project>…` + `project_id`) — it's captured solely in `original_filename` at upload (`sa_keys.py:40`), which was already stored but never surfaced. Chosen approach (option 1 of 3 offered): surface `original_filename`; rejected wiring the existing nullable `label` column (manual entry, unnecessary since the filename already carries the Gmail) and a download `Content-Disposition` filename (separate concern, not asked).
**Files:** `web/src/lib/sa-key-label.ts` (new), `web/src/lib/sa-key-label.test.ts` (new), `web/src/components/fleet/sa-keys-panel.tsx`.
**Verified:** RED-proven pure test (`npx tsx src/lib/sa-key-label.test.ts` → ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND before impl) → GREEN; sibling FE pure tests pass; `tsc -p tsconfig.app.json --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` ✓ 2381 modules. Rendered against the 4 real remaining keys: `ilmcpe22@gmail.com.json`→`ilmcpe22@gmail.com` (×4 gmail keys correct), `key.json`→project-id fallback correct.
**Notes:** **FE-only, no backend/schema change** — `original_filename` was already in the `_meta` payload + `SaKey` type. No generation impact (pure display), so tsc+build+unit is the proof bar (no smoke). `web/dist` gitignored — rebuilt locally; hard-refresh to see it. Same session as the `unique-spirit-494018-h5`/`abdscorpion0` key removal (that one had ONE zero — a different account from the user's `abdscorpion00`). **Also folded in (pre-existing bug, same commit):** the host-assignment `<select>` is the app's one raw native `<select>` (rest of the app uses Radix) styled `bg-white/[0.05] text-white/80` — its `<option>`s rendered white-on-white/invisible in the OS-native dropdown popup (only the highlighted row was legible). Fixed by giving each `<option>` explicit `bg-neutral-900 text-white`. Not caused by the relabel (only option TEXT changed there) — surfaced by it.

## [0137] Worker registry retention cut 2h → 10min — 2026-07-14 (Nggaev-v2 direct)
**What:** `config.worker_registry_prune_seconds` 7200 → **600**. This is the age threshold at which the periodic worker sweep (`worker.py:662`, `workers_repo.prune_stale`) DELETES `workers` rows whose `last_heartbeat` is stale. Because `pc_id = hostname:pid`, every worker restart mints a NEW row, so dead rows accumulate; at 2h a dead host lingered on the fleet page (and, transitively, in the SA-keys host-assignment panel, whose host list = `Set(workers.pc_id.split(':')[0])`) for up to two hours after death.
**Why:** User asked to clear dead hosts within 10 min instead of 2h. Live diagnosis showed 38 `workers` rows collapsing to 13 hostnames, 6 genuinely online (heartbeat <120s) and 7 dead-but-unpruned (oldest ~113min, just under the old 7200s window). 600s clears a dead host ~10min after its last heartbeat.
**Files:** `app/config.py` (+ updated the "2h is >>" comment).
**Verified:** `settings.worker_registry_prune_seconds == 600`; safety ordering intact — prune(600) >> registry_stale(90) and > reclaim_stale(120), so no live worker (heartbeats every 30s) and no mid-job worker (claim reclaimed at 120s) can ever be pruned. `tests/services/test_budget_monitor.py` 21/0 (only place that references the setting; it sets its own mock value, doesn't assert the default). No migration.
**Notes:** Read at process import + the prune runs inside the WORKER sweep, so it takes effect once a worker restarts on this code; fleet-wide needs each box to pull+restart (or set `WORKER_REGISTRY_PRUNE_SECONDS` in that box's `.env`). **Tradeoff (accepted by user):** the SA-keys assignment panel derives its host list from live `workers`, so a host offline >10min now also drops off that panel — its key *assignment* survives (separate hostname-keyed `sa_key_assignments` table) but it can't be seen/scrubbed there until it re-registers. Related: the panel has no liveness cue of its own (unlike the Fleet cards which dim offline + show "last seen") — offered to add one, not taken.

## [0138] SA-keys panel: per-host online/offline status column — 2026-07-14 (Nggaev-v2 direct)
**What:** Added a **Status** column to the SA-keys host-assignment table so you can see which hosts are up before assigning a key. Each row shows the Fleet-cards online dot (green `ONLINE_GREEN` when up, grey when not) + "online" or "last seen Xm ago"; offline rows are dimmed (`opacity-50`); the section header gains an `N/M online` count. Extracted the liveness helpers into a shared pure module `web/src/lib/host-liveness.ts` — `ONLINE_GREEN`, `ago()` (both MOVED verbatim out of `worker-cards.tsx`, which now imports them — DRY, no behavior change), and new `hostLiveness(workers)` which collapses the many `hostname:pid` rows per host into one entry: **online if ANY restart-row is fresh**, `lastHeartbeat` = the most-recent. The panel's host list switched from `Set(workers.pc_id.split(':')[0])` to `hostLiveness(...)`, and every row reference migrated `h` → `h.host`.
**Why:** User: "add a status section to the hosts so I know which are online before assigning keys." The panel previously rendered every host flatly with no liveness cue (unlike the Fleet worker-cards), so a host dead 100min looked identical to a live one — the gap flagged in [[0136]]/the 2h→10min prune discussion ([[0137]]).
**Files:** `web/src/lib/host-liveness.ts` (new), `web/src/lib/host-liveness.test.ts` (new), `web/src/components/fleet/sa-keys-panel.tsx`, `web/src/components/fleet/worker-cards.tsx`.
**Verified:** RED-proven pure test → GREEN (hostLiveness: dedup by hostname, online-if-any, most-recent heartbeat, empty→empty; `ago` null/unparseable→"—", near-now→"just now"); sibling FE tests pass; `tsc -p tsconfig.app.json --noEmit` clean; `npm run build` ✓ 2382 modules. Live-data cross-check (`bool_or(heartbeat >= now()-90s)` grouped by host) matched the render exactly: 7 online (Host-20/23/25/26/35/46 + Oliver) / 5 offline; Host-63 already pruned by the new 10-min window.
**Notes:** FE-only, no backend — `Worker` payload already carries `online` + `last_heartbeat`. No generation impact → tsc+build+unit is the bar. `web/dist` rebuilt locally; hard-refresh. `ago()`/`ONLINE_GREEN` are now shared (worker-cards + sa-keys-panel) — future fleet time-formatting should import from `host-liveness.ts`.

## [0139] Batch rollup denominator — derive launch scope from member jobs (BE-03) — 2026-07-15 (feat/batch-target-denominator)
**What:** Fixed audit finding BE-03: `rollup_for_batch` counted the WHOLE book's TOC rows as the denominator (via a synthetic `not_started = book_total − launched` key), so a lesson-only launch (the default since #89) could never read `complete` — live example, G8-Algebra UZ sat 31/31 done while Monitor reported "18 not started." **GK2-locked mechanism: derive, don't persist.** `rollup_for_batch` now tallies ONLY the batch's launched lessons — `SELECT DISTINCT ON (toc_entry_id) … FROM homework_jobs WHERE batch_id = :id` (already how the tally read the DB) IS the launch scope; no new `batch_targets` table, no migration. This makes every historical batch honest immediately (the scope was always implicit in the jobs already there) — the rejected alternative (a persisted targets table + three-path dual-write) was the exact family that bit #133 (resume forgot to stamp `batch_id`); its own backfill would have had to be seeded FROM the jobs, proving the derivation was already the source of truth. New `toc_total_for_batch` (whole-book TOC row count) is exposed as a separate, display-only `toc_total` field — never folded into the denominator; net **−1 query per batch row** (replaces the old 2-query book_id-lookup + count with one COUNT+JOIN). **Semantic change (flagged for gate, approved):** `complete` = `sum(rollup.values()) > 0 and rollup["done"] == sum(rollup.values())` — every *launched* lesson must be `done`; a launched lesson left `failed`/`cancelled` now blocks `complete` too (previously only in-flight `pending`/`running`/`cancelling` blocked it). GK2: "a halted batch isn't a complete batch"; resume is the operator's path out. `list_jobs` rows (the batch lesson list) each gain `toc_class` — the pure classifier's tag (lesson/header/test/revision/practice/other), computed once over the fetched rows — so the FE renders un-launched/excluded rows with their real class chip instead of a bare "not started" (counted in nothing); a "N book rows not in launch" context line appears under the rollup bar. The `not_started` key is gone from the API and FE entirely (`BatchRollup` type narrowed, `STATUS_ORDER`/`STATUS_COLOR` entry dropped).
**Why:** Filed as BE-03 in the root audit; the lesson-filter default (#89) turned "some book rows are never launch targets" from an edge case into the common case, exposing the whole-book-denominator bug on essentially every campaign batch.
**Files:** `app/repositories/batches.py` (`rollup_for_batch` rewritten, new `toc_total_for_batch`, `list_with_rollups` +`toc_total`, `list_jobs` +`toc_class` via `app.services.toc_classifier.classify_entries`), `app/api/v1/batch.py` (`_rollup_payload` +`toc_total` kwarg, `complete`/`lessons_covered` formulas, threaded at `launch_batch`/`list_batches`/`get_batch`), `web/src/lib/types.ts` (`BatchRollup` drops `not_started`, `BatchSummary` +`toc_total`, batch-jobs row +`toc_class`), `web/src/components/fleet/status.ts`, `batch-lesson-list.tsx`, `batch-funnel.tsx`, `web/src/lib/monitor-grouping.test.ts` (fixture fix). Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-15-batch-target-denominator.md`.
**Verified:** RED→GREEN per task on real-DB scratch (`edu_scratch_be03`) + default suite; suite 1547 passed / 217 skipped (+1 skip = the new real-DB-gated test) across all 4 code tasks, no regression. Read-only $0 acceptance on the live head: `GET /jobs/batches` for the G8-Algebra UZ batch shows `complete: true`, rollup with no `not_started` key, `toc_total` = its book's row count.
**Notes — two brief errata caught by implementers, worth fixing in the plan source:** (1) the plan's illustrative "TAKRORLASH (all-caps, single-page) → header" classifier example is wrong — `toc_classifier`'s keyword pass (pass 1) fires before the containment pass, and "takrorlash" is literally in `_REVISION_KEYWORDS`, so that title classifies `revision`, not `header` (genuine `header` only comes from pass 2's multi-page containment check); task 3's implementer built the test fixture around the real containment mechanism instead. (2) the plan's task-4 exit bar `npx vitest run && tsc --noEmit && npm run build` is unachievable as literally written — `web/src/lib/*.test.ts` files are plain `node:assert` scripts (no `describe`/`it`), and `vitest` isn't even a declared devDependency; vitest reports "no test suite found" for all 7 files including untouched ones (confirmed pre-existing via `git stash`). The substantive bar was proven instead via direct `npx tsx <file>` execution (all 6 affected/adjacent files print `OK`). Both are pre-existing brief/tooling gaps, not regressions introduced by this branch.

## [0140] Clodex OpenAI-compatible API provider — 2026-07-15 (feat/clodex-api-provider)
**What:** Corrected the provisional OpenAI-provider branch after live credential tracing proved the supplied key belongs to `https://clodex.xyz/v1`. Added `clodex` as an **API-only** provider using the `openai` compatibility SDK, dedicated `CLODEX_API_KEY` auth, fixed default base URL (optional `CLODEX_BASE_URL` override), five live text models, typed missing-auth errors, cached/reasoning token normalization, and requested/served model metadata (the endpoint can remap aliases). Added API-only transport validation, extract-role rejection (vision fallbacks require CLI), worker capability/claim gating, fail-safe pricing for all selectable and unknown served aliases, frontend API pin/no-credential blocking, usage styling, docs, and a credential-safe probe script. The ledger attributes Clodex usage to the provider-reported served model while retaining the requested model in raw audit metadata.
**Why:** Reusing `OPENAI_API_KEY` for arbitrary compatible endpoints was both incorrect and unsafe: it could forward a real OpenAI credential to a third party. Clodex is now an explicit trust domain and never reads that variable. `codex` remains the separate CLI/subscription provider.
**Verified:** Backend suite **1572 passed / 217 skipped** after review follow-up; focused provider/accounting suite **138 passed**. `npm ci`, the repository-owned `npm test` command (pinned `tsx`), TypeScript check, and Vite production build pass. Bounded live call through `api_transport.generate(provider="clodex", model="gpt-5.6-luna")` with prompt `Reply with exactly: CLODEX_OK` returned `CLODEX_OK` (13 prompt / 8 output / 21 total tokens); response reported served model `gpt-5.6-terra`, proving the metadata requirement. No migration.

## [0141] Notion textbook fetching correctness (BE-19) — 2026-07-16 (feat/notion-fetch-correctness)
**What:** Fixed the audit's 6 confirmed Notion-fetch failure modes (root `Wishlist.md` BE-19, subsumes BE-08/BE-09). (1) `_pdf_rank` no longer misranks a bot-downloaded workbook as a textbook: bot/source handles (`@elektron_darslikbot`-style) are stripped before ranking, workbook markers are checked before textbook markers, and RU markers (`учебник`/`тетрадь`) are now recognized. (2) New `textbook_candidates(client, page_id)` enumerates EVERY PDF reachable from a subject page — direct blocks, PDFs nested in toggle/column containers (bounded depth), and PDFs on one level of `child_page`s — as `{page_id, block_id, filename, rank, url}`; `available_languages`'s `parts[]` now carries each part's `candidates`. (3) `download_textbook(client, page_id, block_id=None)` takes an explicit selector, or auto-picks the sole best-rank-tier candidate; ties raise `AmbiguousTextbook` → a structured 422 (`{error:"ambiguous_textbook", message, candidates:[{block_id,filename,rank}]}`); a stale/unmatched `block_id` raises a distinct `StaleSelector` 422 (not the generic empty-page message). (4) `/from-notion` prepare now validates: `grade` must be omitted or `"1"`–`"11"`; an explicit grade also walks the subject page's parent chain (subject page → language container → grade page → `notion_lessons_root`, ≤4 hops) confirming it matches the claimed grade+language, catching duplicate-container trees; a missing page → 404, any other Notion error → 502 (one shared mapper covers the ancestry walk and the download step). (5) New pure `app/services/pdf_lang.py` (`detect_pdf_script`, pypdf-only, ≤5 pages sampled) hard-blocks a confident language mismatch after download (`ru`→cyrillic, `uz`/`en`→latin expected) with a 422 naming the file+script, and downgrades to an advisory `warnings[]` entry for a scanned/no-text-layer PDF instead of blocking. (6) FE: a candidate picker on both prepare paths (upload + launcher) surfaces ambiguous best-tier ties, always sends a resolved `block_id`, toasts response `warnings`, and `api.ts` now tolerates BOTH 422 detail shapes (string vs the ambiguity dict) — #86's clicked-page authority preserved throughout.
**Flagged for the gate — semantic refinement beyond the plan's literal rule:** the plan's language guard was a bare `uz`/`en`↔latin, `ru`↔cyrillic check. Two review rounds found this false-positives on language-FAMILY subjects whose textbook content-script is fixed by what the subject *teaches*, not by the container it's fetched under — "Rus tili" under the `uz` container is legitimately Cyrillic-heavy; "Английский язык"/"Узб. яз" under the `ru` container are legitimately Latin-heavy. Fix: `_LANGUAGE_SUBJECT_CONTENT_SCRIPT` (`books.py`) maps `russian→cyrillic`, `english→latin`, `ona-tili→latin` (all `family="languages"`, hygiene-tested against `subjects.REGISTRY`); a mismatch downgrades to advisory **iff** it matches the subject's own content script in **either direction** — literacy subjects (`adabiyot`/`oqish-savodxonligi`/`alifbe`) are deliberately excluded (their content script always follows the container). Intentionally **tightened**, not just loosened: `russian` + `language="ru"` + detected **latin** still hard-422s (contradicts both the container AND the subject's own content script = a genuinely wrong book).
**Review-loop catches (doctrine working as intended):** task 3 review turned the ambiguity 422 from a joined prose string into the structured `{error,message,candidates}` shape FE actually needs to parse (also split `StaleSelector` out from the generic empty-page `NoTextbook` so the two 422s read distinctly). Task 4 review added 2 missing `verify_page_ancestry` branch tests the first pass skipped (no-parent chain-end, and root-mismatch after an otherwise-clean chain) — both are exactly the failure shapes an ancestry check exists to catch. Task 5 review caught the Rus-tili false-positive (fix 1) and then its mirror in the OTHER direction — english/ona-tili under the `ru` container (fix 2) — generalizing a one-subject patch into the `_LANGUAGE_SUBJECT_CONTENT_SCRIPT` map above.
**Plan errata:** 1 documented — task 2's brief named `app/api/v1/books.py`'s route as the consumer of `available_languages`'s shape; the implementer verified the real consumer is `app/api/v1/notion.py` (`books.py`'s `/from-notion` route never touches `available_languages`/`parts` at all). No test/behavior impact, corrected in the task-2 report.
**Live-acceptance catch (fixed in-lane before merge):** the read-only live acceptance run (real Notion workspace, zero writes) found `verify_page_ancestry`'s grade-title regex assumed the GRADE page is titled `"N-sinf"`-style — same convention as its uz language container one level below — mirroring a stale `config.py` doc-comment. The live workspace actually titles grade pages `"N Grade"` (English); `"N-sinf"` is only the uz **language container**'s title, one hop down. As written, every prepare call with an explicit grade would have 422'd on hop 2 (grade-title mismatch) against the real tree. Corrected in a parallel commit (regex + `config.py` comment) before merge, confirmed against the live hierarchy: lessons root → `"N Grade"` pages → language containers (`"N-sinf"` uz / ru equivalent) → subject pages → optional child part pages.
**Verified:** Backend suite **1643 passed / 217 skipped** through task 6 (RED→GREEN per task, no regressions), **1648/217** after the live-acceptance fix commit (`ada9255`, +1 net new test pinning the legacy "N-sinf" grade title still works alongside the live "N Grade" shape); FE `notion-parts.test.ts` (`tsx`, not vitest) green, `tsc --noEmit` + `npm run build` clean. Read-only live acceptance against the real Notion workspace (task 7, $0, no writes) re-verified the audit's confirmed shapes with the new crawler — G1-UZ Matematika's 4 child-page parts and G11-UZ Algebra's 2 same-page parts both now enumerate as candidates — and ran `detect_pdf_script` on the two G6-RU PDFs (Part-1 Uzbek→latin→would-block for `ru`, Part-2→cyrillic→passes), plus caught the grade-title regex bug above. No migration. Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-16-notion-fetch-correctness.md`. Closes wishlist `notion-pdf-rank-markers-1`, `notion-nested-part-pages-1`; `notion-crosslang-part-picker-1` verified still open (a distinct cross-language ask BE-19 didn't build — see WISHLIST for the honest split).

## [0142] Fleet-wide per-credential api concurrency limiter (BE-16 / `concurrency-knob-1` Phase 2) — 2026-07-16 (feat/credential-rate-limit)
**What:** Supplemented the per-process concurrency cap on `transport=api` calls with a **fleet-wide, Postgres-backed per-credential slot limiter**, closing the audit's BE-16 finding (`concurrency-knob-1` Phase 2 — the reactive-backoff half shipped 2026-06-25 already, but N worker processes sharing one Vertex project could still multiply the per-process cap). Six TDD tasks, each already reviewed and merged onto this branch before this finishing task: (1) migration 0047 — `credential_slots(id, credential, pc_id, acquired_at)` + `sa_keys.max_concurrent_calls` (nullable int, CHECK `>=1`); (2) `credential_id.credential_for(provider, env)` — pure fingerprinting, gemini precedence mirrors `api_transport._gemini_client` exactly (`GEMINI_API_KEY` checked first, then the Vertex SA pair → `gemini:{project}`), else `{provider}:{sha256(key)[:16]}` for claude/clodex; (3) `credential_limiter.acquire`/`release`/`sweep` — `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext(credential))` + count-then-insert in one transaction (TOCTOU-safe), exponential poll backoff (1s→5s+jitter), `STALE_TTL_SECONDS = 2×per_attempt_timeout_seconds`; (4) `resolve_limit` — per-project override (`MIN` over duplicate `project_id` rows) with ~60s cache (errors never cached), env defaults `CREDENTIAL_MAX_CONCURRENT_GEMINI`/`_CLAUDE`/`_CLODEX` (8 each) + dedicated `credential_slot_wait_seconds` (120s, NOT the 600s per-attempt timeout — a 600s budget would collide with the pipeline's own 600s `wait_for` and exit the leg with no same-provider retry); (5) wired into `agent._spawn_once`'s api branch **inside** the local semaphore (a fleet-slot waiter still counts against the process cap — cli is retired so the local semaphore is process-protection only); acquire-timeout returns a `429`-shaped error consumed by the existing rate-limit retry loop; release via `asyncio.create_task` + `asyncio.shield` (mirrors `worker.py`'s documented double-cancel craft) so a second cancellation can't strand a slot; SDK client timeouts added to `api_transport` (= `per_attempt_timeout_seconds`) so no live call can outlive the TTL; `sa_key_apply.set_credentials_env` now also scrubs `GEMINI_API_KEY` so an operator assignment always wins (flagged behavior change); (6) `PATCH /sa-keys/{id}` (project-wide atomic update + cache eviction) + `GET /sa-keys` `slots_in_use`/`effective_limit` + FE numeric override input + in-flight display.
**Why:** Live incident 2026-07-16 — 12 workers on one Vertex account drove 92–134 concurrent api calls (reconstructed from `agent_usages` timestamps) → 473 rate-limit errors in 2 hours; the shipped reactive backoff absorbs residue but can't prevent the stampede, and 8 workers × `AGENT_MAX_CONCURRENCY=8` still allows 64 concurrent calls per account even after the operator's mitigation. A fleet-wide ceiling — not just a per-process one — was the only fix that actually bounds real concurrent load against a credential.
**Files:** `alembic/versions/0047_credential_slots.py`, `app/services/credential_id.py` (new), `app/services/credential_limiter.py` (new), `app/services/agent.py`, `app/services/api_transport.py`, `app/services/sa_key_apply.py`, `app/services/worker.py`, `app/config.py`, `app/models/sa_key.py`, `app/repositories/sa_keys.py`, `app/api/v1/sa_keys.py`, `web/src/lib/api.ts`, `web/src/lib/types.ts`, `web/src/components/fleet/sa-keys-panel.tsx`, plus matching test files (`tests/migrations/test_0047_credential_slots.py`, `tests/services/test_credential_id.py`, `tests/services/test_credential_limiter_resolve.py`, `tests/integration/test_credential_limiter.py`, `tests/services/test_agent_limiter_wiring.py`, `tests/services/test_api_transport.py`, `tests/services/test_sa_key_apply_core.py`, `tests/services/test_worker_credential_sweep.py`, `tests/api/test_sa_keys_api.py`, `tests/integration/test_sa_keys_repo_concurrency.py`). This finishing task additionally touched `CLAUDE.md`, `docs/HOW_IT_WORKS.md`, `docs/CODE_MAP.md`, `docs/DATABASE.md`, `.env.example`, `docs/memory/WISHLIST.md`, `docs/memory/MASTER_MEMORY.md`, `docs/memory/INDEX.md`. Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-16-credential-rate-limit.md` (→ `shipped/` at finish). Root-level (untracked, not committed — see Notes) `Wishlist.md` BE-16 entry marked addressed with this commit's sha.
**Verified:** Rebase onto `origin/Nggaev-v2` (picked up #96 BE-19 + the codex-second-pass plan-doc fold) was a clean fast-forward-style replay, **zero conflicts** — the plan doc now matches origin's amended version byte-for-byte. Full backend suite **1712 passed / 237 skipped** both before and after the docs/worklog commit; FE `tsc -p tsconfig.app.json --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` ✓ 2383 modules. **Live acceptance (task 7, ~$0.01, pre-authorized):** fresh process, `CREDENTIAL_MAX_CONCURRENT_GEMINI=2` exported before the settings singleton imports, `DATABASE_URL` pointed at scratch `edu_scratch_credlim` (already migrated to head 0047) while the real Vertex gemini credential (`GOOGLE_APPLICATION_CREDENTIALS`/`GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT`/`GOOGLE_CLOUD_LOCATION`, presence-checked only, never printed) drove the actual model calls — the correct pairing (the real API call needs real creds; only the slot table needs to be at head, and prod's DB is NOT yet on migration 0047). 6 concurrent `agent.run_phase(provider="gemini", model="gemini-2.5-flash-lite", transport="api", phase_prompt="Reply with exactly: OK", homework_job_id=None, phase_output_id=None)` calls + a 250ms poller on `credential_slots`. Result (two independent runs, both clean PASS): **(a) peak sampled in-flight count == 2 EXACTLY** both runs; **(b) ≥14 distinct nonzero poll observations** (well over the ≥6 bar); **(c) the slowest call's wall time exceeded the fastest call's bare duration by ~4s (run 1) / ~4s (run 2)** — a caller demonstrably waited for a slot; **(d) all 6 calls returned exactly `"OK"`**; slot rows self-cleaned to 0 after each run (verified by direct count query). Full trace + per-call timings in `.superpowers/sdd/task-7-report.md`.
**Notes:** **Operational trap hit during acceptance, worth flagging:** the main checkout's `.env` (sourced for the real Vertex credential) carries `AGENT_MAX_CONCURRENCY=1` — a host-specific tuning value. Sourcing it wholesale into the smoke's env silently capped the LOCAL semaphore to 1 (entered *before* the fleet limiter, by design — see the wire-point note above), which fully masked the fleet ceiling behind a stricter local one and produced an apparent single-file/serialized run (peak=1) on the first attempt. Fixed by explicitly overriding `AGENT_MAX_CONCURRENCY=8` for the smoke process. This is a real footgun for anyone re-running this acceptance shape from a `.env`-sourced shell — the local semaphore is *also* a ceiling and must be sized above the fleet limit being tested, or the fleet limiter's own behavior is unobservable. **Two review cycles already folded in-branch before this task:** a fresh-Fable adversarial review (verdict APPROVE-WITH-FIXES, 8 fixes: C1 credential-precedence parity being the critical one) and a codex second-pass review (verdict request-changes, 8 further fixes: dedicated 120s slot-wait budget instead of reusing the 600s per-attempt timeout, `MIN`-over-duplicate-`project_id` resolution, SDK timeouts to make the STALE_TTL ceiling actually hard, hardened acceptance criteria, shielded double-cancel-safe release, `GEMINI_API_KEY` assignment-scrub, `Field(ge=0)` + CHECK boundary validation) — both folded mid-execution, confirmed by diffing the plan doc against `origin/Nggaev-v2`'s copy (identical after rebase). **Worklog numbering:** the controller pinned this entry to **0142** even though the INDEX tail read 0141 at rebase time and an unmerged sibling PR (#97, "per-model Vertex location router") had already reserved 0143 for itself — this lane's plan committed 0142 first, so the number holds regardless of merge order; re-verified the INDEX tail still had no existing 0142 row before writing this entry. **Root `Wishlist.md` handling:** this repo-root audit file is untracked in git everywhere (confirmed: absent from `origin/Nggaev-v2`, and the immediately-prior BE-19 lane [[0141]] referenced-but-never-committed it too) — it's a session-spanning local scratch audit trail, not a versioned backlog doc. Followed that established precedent: edited the closure note directly into the **main checkout's** copy (not this worktree, not committed to this branch) rather than importing the whole ~33KB multi-audit document into a feature branch for one line. Flagged this judgment call for GK2 review. Flagged for the gate (unchanged from the plan): fail-open-on-limiter-DB-error is an availability-over-enforcement choice; a fleet-slot wait holds the local semaphore slot; default ceilings (8/8/8) are operator-tunable starting points, not verified Vertex quotas; `sa_keys.max_concurrent_calls` binds by `project_id`, so env-key credentials (claude/clodex/AI-Studio) get provider defaults only; all 8 local semaphore slots can in theory fill with gemini fleet-slot waiters and delay claude/clodex calls in the same process (bounded, degrades not deadlocks, accepted).

## 0143 — 2026-07-16 — feat/gemini-location-router — Per-model Vertex location router

`gemini-2.5-flash` began 429ing (`RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED`, instant) on the Vertex **global** endpoint across ALL five pool SA projects (live-probed, 12 minimal calls ≈ $0.001) while serving fine regionally (us-central1/europe-west4); the preview models (3-flash, 3.1-pro) are the inverse — global-only (404 regional). One `GOOGLE_CLOUD_LOCATION` could no longer serve the production mix (extract+judge defaults = 2.5-flash; content 3-flash; solver 3.1-pro), and 19 pending jobs carried frozen 2.5-flash stamps. **Fix: route the Vertex location per model at client construction** — `api_transport._location_for(model)`: env `GEMINI_MODEL_LOCATIONS` (JSON, MERGED over the built-in default `{"gemini-2.5-flash": "us-central1"}`) → `GOOGLE_CLOUD_LOCATION` → `global`; malformed env JSON logs an error and falls back (never raises); `GEMINI_API_KEY` branch untouched; cli path untouched. Rejected alternative: flipping launch_defaults to 3-flash/2.5-pro (judge=3-flash would self-grade-swap to claude-opus-4-7 via `model_tiers.resolve_judge`; judge=2.5-pro ≈ 4× judge cost ≈ +$200-400 for October; and the 19 pending jobs would need restamping — the router needs NO restamp since stamps pin model, not location). 6 new tests (RED-by-construction: new function + changed signature); acceptance = the production `_gemini()` path live: 2.5-flash routed→us-central1 `'OK'` (was 429 same morning), 3-flash global `'OK'` (~$0.002). **Deploy: fleet pull+restart required** — workers make the extract/judge calls; the 19 pending jobs recover only after that. Process note: implementer subagent lost to repeated API-529s after GREEN; controller verified diff vs plan, ran suite, committed. (0142 = the BE-16 limiter plan lane, in flight.)

## [0145] Book deletion integrity — batches-FK fix, ingest/active-job guards, book-scoped activation/delete locks, disk cleanup (BE-02) — 2026-07-16 (feat/book-delete-integrity)
**What:** Closed root-audit finding BE-02 (`DELETE /books/{id}` was unsafe on three fronts) across 4 build tasks, gated twice by the user's external reviewer before execution (ingest guard, all-five-activation-path locking, a strict race oracle, and the 0145 worklog pin all came out of the second gate pass). (1) **Batches-in-transaction fix** — `books_repo.delete` was reproducing the audit's `batches_book_id_fkey` 500: `batches.book_id` carries no `ondelete` rule, so a book with any batch row raised `IntegrityError` on the book DELETE. Fixed order is now `homework_jobs` (ORM `session.delete`, `phase_outputs` FK-cascade) → `batches` (explicit `sa_delete`) → the `books` row (`toc_entries` FK-cascades off that). `agent_usages` is the deliberate exception — its book/job/phase FKs are `ondelete=SET NULL`, so billing/audit rows survive with those FKs nulled. (2) **Route guards** — `DELETE /books/{id}` now 404s on a missing book, then 409s while `status` is `uploading`/`toc_extracting` ("still being ingested… wait for it to finish or fail, then delete" — the live TOC extractor is still reading the on-disk PDF) or while `jobs_repo.count_active_for_book` (new: `pending`/`running`/`cancelling`) is nonzero ("cancel the active job(s) or their batch first") — deliberately worded with **no assumption the book belongs to a batch** (bare `/generate`-only books are guarded identically). (3) **Book-scoped advisory locks** — a Postgres lock keyed `pg_advisory_xact_lock[_shared](hashtext("book:<id>"))` closes the race between the delete guard-read and any of the **five activation paths**: `POST /generate`, job retry, batch launch, batch resume, and TOC retry each take the **SHARED** form before their own guard read (so concurrent activators never block each other, but a delete does); `DELETE /books/{id}` takes the **EXCLUSIVE** form before its 404 fetch. The identity-map discovery, found BY the race tests: `Session.get()`/`jobs_repo.get()` silently hands back the SAME pre-lock in-memory object on a "re-fetch" unless the session first calls `session.expire(obj)` — without it, a job/batch/book deleted-out-from-under a waiting activator surfaced as a `StaleDataError` on the eventual UPDATE instead of a clean 404; the three fetch-then-lock paths (job retry, batch resume, TOC retry) expire-then-re-fetch; /generate and batch launch lock BEFORE their first read, so there is no pre-lock object to expire. 12 winner-conditional race-oracle tests (`tests/integration/test_book_delete_race.py`), including the real-route TOC-retry win-side driven through the actual endpoint with a positive spawn assertion (added in a task-3 review-fix cycle, narrowing an overly broad `except`). (4) **Post-commit disk cleanup + honest FE copy** — `shutil.rmtree(storage.book_dir(book_id))` runs strictly after commit (a rolled-back delete must never destroy files), best-effort: a missing dir is a silent no-op, any other failure is `logger.error`-logged but never turns an already-committed delete into an error response (accepted: orphan dir + ERROR log only, no sweeper). `web/src/routes/library.tsx`'s confirm dialog now says plainly that the PDF and all generated content are permanently removed.
**Why:** Root-audit BE-02: deleting a book could 500 on the batches FK, could race a concurrently-spawning job/retry/launch against files and rows the delete was removing, and gave no on-disk cleanup or honest warning of what "delete" actually destroys.
**Files:** `app/repositories/books.py` (`lock_book_shared`/`lock_book_exclusive`, `delete` reordered), `app/repositories/jobs.py` (`count_active_for_book`), `app/api/v1/books.py` (`DELETE /{id}` guards+lock+cleanup, TOC retry lock), `app/api/v1/jobs.py` (`/generate` + retry lock), `app/api/v1/batch.py` (launch + resume lock), `web/src/routes/library.tsx` (confirm copy); tests: `tests/api/test_delete_book_guards.py`, `tests/integration/test_book_delete_race.py`, `tests/integration/test_books_repo_delete.py`, `tests/integration/test_delete_book_guard_db.py`, `tests/api/conftest.py` (+fixtures). Docs this finishing task: `docs/HOW_IT_WORKS.md` (delete flow: guard order, lock scheme, deletion order, retained tables), `docs/DATABASE.md` (books deletion-semantics note), `docs/CODE_MAP.md` (`books.py` route + repo bullets). Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/shipped/2026-07-16-book-delete-integrity.md` (gated twice — ingest guard, 5-path locks, strict oracle, and this worklog number all came out of the second pass).
**Verified:** Full suite green through all 4 tasks (real-DB-gated race/lock tests included); this finishing task's rebase check found `origin/Nggaev-v2` unmoved (`git log HEAD..origin/Nggaev-v2` empty) — no rebase needed. Zero model calls anywhere in this lane ($0) — book-deletion integrity is pure DB/route/lock plumbing, no LLM surface.
**Notes — flagged for the gate (unchanged from the plan):** (1) deleting a fully-generated book still destroys its `phase_outputs` (the deliverable) — now guarded+honestly-confirmed but still irreversible from the DB's perspective; a pushed Notion archive is the only surviving copy. (2) a cleanup failure leaves an orphan dir with only an ERROR log — accepted (disk-only, no sweeper). (3) the 409 counts `cancelling` as active (a job mid-cancel still holds a worker) — deliberate. **Worklog numbering:** controller-pinned to **0145** (0144 reserved by the separate, at-this-time-unmerged prepare-status lane); INDEX tail was 0143 at rebase-check time (0144 not yet present), so this entry leaves an expected 0144 gap rather than colliding — re-verified no existing 0145 row before writing. This lane does **not** open or merge a PR; GK2 runs the final whole-branch review/gate.

## [0147] SA-key dead-host management + restart-proof scrub — 2026-07-17 (feat/sa-key-dead-host)
**What:** Two verified bugs from an external review. (1) **UI**: the SA-keys panel host table was built ONLY from the worker registry (`hostLiveness(workers)`), whose rows are pruned 10 min after the last heartbeat, while `sa_key_assignments` persists — a dead host's assignment became unreachable ("3 workers" on a key, nothing to unassign, key un-deletable through the 409). Fixed with a pure union module `web/src/lib/sa-key-hosts.ts`: `assignmentHosts(liveness, assignments)` = registry hosts ∪ assignment-only hostnames (offline placeholder rows, `assignmentOnly: true`), plus `assignmentOnlyStatus(registryState)` → `gone`/`checking`/`registry unavailable` so "gone" only renders off a *successful* `/workers` response (no false flash while loading; registry errors surface distinctly). Panel iterates the union; Unassign/Scrub work on dead hosts (backend routes always accepted any hostname — pure display fix). (2) **Worker**: a restarted worker skipped a pending scrub — the branch gated on in-memory `_applied_key_sha`, `None` on boot — so "Scrub" looked done while the returning machine kept `active.json` + env credentials and billed the revoked key. Now a **four-source residue gate**: sha ∪ `sa_key_active_path().exists()` ∪ either `GOOGLE_*` var present in `os.environ` (presence, not truthiness) ∪ credential line in the worker `.env` (new pure `sa_key_apply.env_file_has_credentials`). Idle-gate kept; still a no-op on clean hosts (no per-heartbeat churn from the persistent scrub row). (3) **Honest copy**: scrub toast → "Scrub requested; applies when the host returns and is idle."; label `(scrubbed)` → `SCRUB REQUESTED` (the API only records the request). Final-review Important fixed in-branch: Unassign was `disabled` for scrub rows (`!a?.key_id`, but scrub nulls `key_id`) — the tombstone would have been permanently undismissable; now `!a`.
**Design:** worker self-ack (delete own assignment row post-clear) REJECTED — assignments are keyed by hostname; two worker processes on one host share the row and the first clear would erase the instruction before the second saw it. The scrub row is a deliberate tombstone; the operator dismisses it via Unassign (now possible for dead hosts). No migration, no backend route changes.
**Files:** `app/services/worker.py` (scrub branch), `app/services/sa_key_apply.py` (+`env_file_has_credentials`), `tests/services/test_worker_sa_key_sync.py` (+13), `web/src/lib/sa-key-hosts.ts`+`.test.ts` (new), `web/src/components/fleet/sa-keys-panel.tsx`.
**Verified:** genuine RED via pre-fix file swap — 4/5 isolated residue-matrix cases fail on old code (sha-only stays green: old behavior); GREEN 16/16 worker tests; suite **1785 passed / 274 skipped**; FE tsx tests + tsc + build clean. Plan gated 3 rounds by the user's external reviewer (honest-status copy, no-gone-flash, 4-source predicate, per-branch test isolation all folded pre-code).
**Operator note:** hosts scrubbed while down need a worker pull+restart to actually clear (startup `_sync_sa_key` now does it); the `SCRUB REQUESTED` row stays until dismissed via Unassign — that's the standing revoke instruction, not a bug.
**Gate review round 2 (4 findings, all verified in code, all fixed in-branch before merge):** (1) *Important* — scrub was process-idle, not host-idle: an idle worker process could clear the shared `active.json`/`.env` while a **sibling** process on the same host (embedded + standalone share one hostname) was mid-job (`self._tasks` is process-local). New `jobs_repo.count_running_for_host(hostname)` (`claimed_by` is `hostname:pid`, matched by `split_part(claimed_by,':',1)=hostname` — no LIKE/prefix over-match) gates the destructive clear on host-wide idleness, checked in the already-open session on the rare scrub-with-residue path; DB error there defers (fail-safe). Extracted to `_scrub_if_idle`. (2) *Important* — the scrub path's `.env` read + file mutations ran outside any exception boundary; a malformed `.env` (UnicodeDecodeError) propagated out of `_sync_sa_key`, which runs at **startup before the main loop's guard** → worker crash. Scrub detection+cleanup now run inside `_sync_sa_key`'s try/except (apply path byte-identical; `asg` is a plain dict, safe after session close). (3) *Important* — `unassignSaKey`/`scrubSaKey` discarded the response; `authFetch` never throws on 4xx/5xx (only clears token on 401) → success toast on failure. Both now check `res.ok`+throw (house pattern). (4) *Moderate* — scrub `onSuccess` invalidated only `sa-key-assignments`, not `sa-keys` (whose `worker_count` drives the pool count + Delete button); now invalidates both, and the assignment `<select>` went from uncontrolled `defaultValue` → controlled `value` so it tracks server state after a scrub/unassign refetch. RED-proven: both new worker tests fail on pre-fix code (sibling-busy clears; malformed `.env` raises the exact UnicodeDecodeError); `count_running_for_host` SQL proven on real Postgres incl. the hostname-prefix boundary. Suite **1787 passed / 275 skipped**; internal whole-branch re-review Ready-to-merge, zero regressions. Considered-and-declined micro-nit: the shared scrub `except` uses `logger.warning` not `logger.exception` — kept, because that `except` also wraps benign transient assignment-read DB blips where per-blip tracebacks would be log noise, and every failure mode there is a safe deferral. Commits `177e145` (findings 1+2) + `6f441f4` (findings 3+4).
**Gate review round 3 (finding 1 re-opened — the round-2 fix was a snapshot, not synchronization; corrected via a 7-commit sub-lane, plan `2026-07-17-scrub-claim-synchronization.md`, gated 3 more rounds before code):** round-2's `count_running_for_host` snapshot had a TOCTOU (a sibling could claim between the count and the file delete), no claim-gate (the host kept claiming while a scrub was pending, so the revoke never converged), and counted only `running` (missed `cancelling`). Corrected with the **BE-02 advisory-lock pattern, host-scoped** (`hashtext("host:{hostname}")`): (a) `workers_repo.lock_host_shared/exclusive` primitives; (b) **claim-gate** — `_claim_one` takes the SHARED host lock at the top of its claim tx and re-reads the tombstone (`sa_keys_repo.scrub_pending_for_host`) UNDER it, refusing the claim if a scrub is pending → the host **parks** (claims nothing) and in-flight jobs drain; (c) **all three assignment-state write routes** (assign/unassign/scrub) take the EXCLUSIVE host lock before writing, or a claim that already re-read "no tombstone" could still create a replacement after the tombstone commits; (d) **scrub-clear** takes the EXCLUSIVE lock and re-reads `scrub_pending_for_host` (abort if a concurrent assign/unassign dismissed it) + `count_active_for_host` == 0 (**running+cancelling**, renamed) UNDER the lock, then clears; (e) **tx-ownership fix** — the explicit `session.begin()` moved from `_scrub_if_idle` to its caller `_sync_sa_key` (a nested begin would raise "a transaction is already begun" since `get_assignment_with_key`'s SELECT autobegins); the apply path stays outside so its `to_thread` key-pull holds no DB connection; (f) UI — label `SCRUB REQUESTED · HOST PARKED`, and a `window.confirm` on Unassign of a tombstoned host (honest copy: dismissing cancels the revoke and, mid-drain, leaves the old key applied — the clear will NOT complete). **Guarantee delivered:** in-flight running/cancelling jobs finish/cancel on their own (never killed), no replacements are claimed while parked, and the clear never interleaves with a live claim. Lock ordering is safe (host lock never co-occurs with the BE-02 book/section locks; advisory-lock-always-first within a tx). **Verified:** genuine RED on the claim-gate, the tombstone-cleared-under-lock abort, and a lock-order oracle (lock→re-reads→clear); the winner-conditional two-connection races drive the REAL tombstone write vs `_claim_one`; 16 round-3 real-DB tests pass on real Postgres; default suite **1789 passed / 290 skipped**; whole-branch review "race closed for all interleavings, ready to merge". Known follow-ups filed to WISHLIST (`scrub-park-observability-1` throttled parked-claim log; `scrub-jobless-spawn-drain-1` book-upload TOC extraction isn't a job so it's outside `count_active_for_host`). Commits `d6feff7`/`7270cd0`/`a86b531`/`e991ecf`/`0c3ffc3`/`17b82f1` + review fixes `0a5cbeb`. **Fleet-rollout caveat:** the `HOST PARKED` label shows as soon as the tombstone lands, but a worker still on pre-round-3 code has no claim-gate and keeps claiming until the fleet pulls — the #93 version-floor is the enforcement lever.
## [0146] Post-#100 follow-up: adjacent book mutations take the shared lock — 2026-07-17 (Nggaev-v2 direct)
**What:** External post-merge review of BE-02 (#100) found the four sibling mutation routes — `POST /toc/accept`, `PATCH /books/{id}`, `PATCH /toc/{entry}`, `DELETE /toc/{entry}` — mutate book-scoped rows WITHOUT the book lock: a `DELETE /books/{id}` committing between their read and their UPDATE surfaced as `StaleDataError` → 500. All four now take `books_repo.lock_book_shared` before their first read (lock-then-read — they know book_id from the path, so no expire dance needed, same shape as `/generate`). New race tests (Part G in `test_book_delete_race.py`): delete-wins → PATCH returns clean 404 (RED-proven: pre-fix, the PATCH does NOT block and races through the delete's open tx); patch-wins → delete then succeeds.
**Why:** #100's final review had triaged these as accept ("500s harmless, out of the five-activation-path contract"); the external reviewer re-rated Medium — a 500 on an operator edit racing an operator delete is avoidable for four one-line locks. Fix pattern already proven on the five activation paths.
**Files:** `app/api/v1/books.py`, `tests/integration/test_book_delete_race.py`.
**Verified:** RED via pre-fix file swap (PATCH fails to block, races the open delete tx); GREEN 14/14 race tests ×2 (flake-free) on scratch `edu_scratch_bookdel`; full default suite **1733 passed / 257 skipped**.
**Notes:** Same review's other findings: (a) fleet workers' locally-cached PDFs (`book_fetch.ensure_book_pdf_sync`) survive book deletion — orphan storage only, filed as WISHLIST `book-delete-worker-pdf-orphans-1`; (b) Biome lint errors at `library.tsx:338` — pre-existing repo-wide lint noise, not a gate (house FE gates are tsc+build), ignored.
## 0144 — 2026-07-16 — feat/prepare-status-redo — Prepare dialog system-aware status + explicit TOC redo

**What:** The "Prepare a subject" dialog's chips used to describe Notion state only (`TEXTBOOK READY`/`no textbook`) — an already-ingested, already-TOC-extracted subject looked identical to a never-touched one, and picking it silently deduped onto the existing book with no way to deliberately redo a stale/replaced TOC. Six TDD tasks: (1) migration `0048_book_notion_sources` — new `book_notion_sources` mapping table (`book_id` FK CASCADE, normalized `notion_page_id`/`notion_block_id` UNIQUE pair, `linked_at`) + `books.toc_ready_at` (nullable, stamped on successful extraction and on `/toc/accept`, cleared on `/toc/retry`); `notion_sources_repo.upsert_link`/`links_for_sources` (batch-loaded, exact page+block match — never page-level, since two candidate PDFs can live on one part page). (2) `POST /books/from-notion` now links the RESOLVED source (the exact candidate `download_textbook` fetched, surfaced via a `DownloadedTextbook` result object replacing a bare tuple) **transactionally** — book creation + link land in one commit on both the fresh-ingest and the SHA-dedup-hit paths, so a post-ingest link failure can never strand an extracting-but-unlinked book. (3) `/toc/retry`'s allowlist gained `toc_ready` (a deliberate redo) behind a structured `409 toc_retry_blocked_by_jobs` (`{message, count, jobs≤20}` — FE never parses prose) when homework jobs still reference the book's sections; **lifecycle split**: `/toc/retry` clears `toc_validation*`/`toc_ready_at` after its guards pass, `POST /toc/accept` PRESERVES the validation audit trail and STAMPS `toc_ready_at` instead. (4) `GET /notion/grades/{id}/available-languages` enriches every crawled candidate server-side (one `links_for_sources` + one `books_repo.get_many` + one grouped-COUNT each for TOC total and blocking jobs — batch-loaded for the WHOLE response, never per-candidate) with `book_id`/`book_status`/`toc_validation`/`toc_total`/`toc_ready_at`/`redo_blocked_by_jobs`, plus a part-level `prepared: true` rollup when exactly one candidate is linked. (5) `lib/prepare-status.ts`'s pure `partPrepareStatus` maps that enrichment to 6 chip states (NO TEXTBOOK/TEXTBOOK READY/PREPARED·N lessons/PREPARING/NEEDS REVIEW/FAILED) + an action set (Use existing / Redo / Review / Retry), consumed by ONE shared `<PrepareStatusPanel>` from both `upload.tsx` and `launcher.tsx` (the BE-19 copy-paste-drift lesson — no second implementation). (6, this task) `scripts/backfill_notion_sources.py` for books that predate the mapping table — re-crawls the tree, downloads+sha256-hashes each candidate, matches by `(content_sha256, subject)` (dry-run by default, `--apply` to write, `--grade N` to scope the download cost); plus two riders and this doc/memory pass.

**GK2 design review, two rounds folded before any code:** round 1 (7 corrections) locked the mapping-table identity model (not a column on `books` — two candidates can live on one page, and SHA-dedup means one book is reachable from several Notion locations) and the six-chip UX. Round 2 (4 corrections, `e33ca89`): `notion_page_id`/`notion_block_id` NOT NULL (block id is always known post-download-resolution); `links_for_sources` takes exact `(page,block)` pairs, never page-level lookups; **transactional linking** (task 2's one-commit requirement above — the original draft had the route link AFTER `ingest_pdf`'s own commit); and the retry/accept **lifecycle split** (task 3 above) replacing a single shared clear-always assumption. All four are load-bearing in the shipped code, not just plan wording.

**Riders (this task):** (1) `web/src/routes/book.tsx` — the book page never surfaced a redo path for an already-`toc_ready` book opened directly (only the Prepare dialog's `PrepareStatusPanel` did), which was the *only* redo path for a two-linked-part subject (see the known gap below) since that part's chip stays a plain `TEXTBOOK READY`. Added a destructive "Redo TOC extraction" button (`window.confirm`-gated, same copy as the panel) next to the section filter whenever the book is steady `toc_ready`; the confirm string is a deliberate **duplicate**, not extracted into a shared helper — one `window.confirm` literal shared across a route file and an unrelated component isn't worth the import/plumbing churn, noted inline for whoever touches the copy next. (2) `web/src/lib/prepare-status.ts`'s `redoBlockedReason` now reads verbatim `"N homework job(s) reference this book's sections — delete the affected sections first"`, matching `app/api/v1/books.py`'s `toc_retry_blocked_by_jobs` 409 `message` field exactly (previously a shorter, differently-worded synthesized string) — an operator sees identical phrasing whether the block comes from the dialog's disabled-redo state or a live retry-call 409.

**Known gap, logged not fixed:** a language part with TWO linked candidates (e.g. the same file uploaded twice under different Notion locations) never gets the part-level `prepared` rollup by design (task 4 — ambiguous which book it now represents) and falls back to a bare `TEXTBOOK READY` chip with no redo/rollup/polling for either linked book. `prepare-two-linked-part-redo-1` added to WISHLIST.

**Pre-existing isolation finding (task 4, not a defect of this branch):** a combined `RUN_DB_INTEGRATION=1 -k run` pass exposed that `tests/integration/test_migration_0045_archived_job.py`'s `asyncio.run` + module-global engine raises "attached to a different loop" when other async DB tests run first in the same process — passes clean in isolation, and the default (non-DB-integration) suite is unaffected either way. Extended the existing `migration-test-shared-db-isolation-1` WISHLIST entry with this second event-loop-bound mode rather than filing a new item (same root class: tests sharing one scratch `DATABASE_URL`/engine across a process).

**Backfill script safety (task 6):** `DATABASE_URL` is read from raw `os.environ` and captured at the top of the module, BEFORE any of the script's own imports (which transitively pull in `app.config`, whose `load_dotenv(override=False)` walks up parent directories via `find_dotenv`) — an unset `DATABASE_URL` refuses to start with a clear message, never silently resolving to whatever `.env`/config default happens to be reachable. This is not a theoretical concern: this worktree itself has no local `.env`, and `load_dotenv`'s upward walk from a nested worktree path resolves to `/Users/macmini5/Documents/.env`, whose `DATABASE_URL` points at `edu_copy` on a different host — empirically confirmed live in this session (capturing `os.environ` before vs. after importing `app.config` shows `None` → the outer `.env`'s value). The pure matching decision (`decide_link` — no-match/ambiguous/would-link/would-repoint/already-linked over `(content_sha256, subject)`) is TDD'd (RED first at every step) in `tests/test_backfill_notion_sources.py`, mirroring `scripts/golden_eval.py`'s existing convention of pure decision helpers living directly in the script module; the download/hash/DB-write glue is smoke-tested via `--help` and an intentionally-unset-`DATABASE_URL` refusal, per the brief's "pure function tests suffice, no scripts-test convention exists" allowance — no live Notion/DB call happens in this task (that's the controller's acceptance step).

**Acceptance fixes (controller review, three follow-up commits in-branch):** (1) `6703f19` fail-clear preflights — the controller's acceptance run hit a raw pydantic traceback on a missing `DATABASE_URL` (the original top-of-module env capture ran too late: module-level `app.*` imports execute first under `python -m`, and with no reachable `.env` the `Settings` import itself raises), fixed structurally by making the module import NOTHING from `app.*` at module level (all heavy imports lazy inside `run()`, after `main()`'s `preflight_database_url`) plus a `to_regclass('book_notion_sources')` migration-0048 preflight that fails one-line/exit-2 BEFORE any Notion download on an un-migrated target DB (verified against a real throwaway un-migrated Postgres). (2) `f22de0b` (controller-authored) sys.path shim so the file-path invocation `python scripts/backfill_notion_sources.py` resolves the deferred `app.*` imports exactly like the documented `-m` form. (3) Review MEDIUM — `decide_link` gained a distinct `would_repoint` action (reason names BOTH books: "was book A -> now book B") so the dry-run report surfaces link MOVES separately from fresh links before `--apply` (which still writes both via the same upsert; the applied line reports "of which N re-pointed"), with the counts/summary/dry-run/applied lines split out and pinned by pure `format_*` helpers + an `ACTIONS`-coverage test.

**Verified:** Backend suite **1758 passed / 254 skipped** at the branch tip (task-6 baseline 1740/254 + 18 script tests accumulated across the initial commit and the acceptance fixes: 7 matching + 7 preflight + 4 report-formatting, zero regressions). FE: `npx tsx src/lib/prepare-status.test.ts` green (updated the two exact-string assertions for the rider-2 wording change), `npx tsc -p tsconfig.app.json --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` ✓. No new migration in this task (0048 shipped in task 1). Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-07-16-prepare-status-toc-redo.md` (→ `shipped/` at finish).

## 0148 — 2026-07-17 — feat/teaching-audit — Teaching-equivalence + learnability audit (closed-book simulated-student exam)

**What:** A new standalone, offline audit tool — `app/services/teaching_audit.py` + CLI `scripts/teaching_audit.py` — that measures whether a generated homework packet actually *teaches what the textbook lesson teaches*, closing a verified gap: the judge and the extract coverage-contract (#84) both grade the packet against itself/its extract, so a packet that silently under-covers the lesson still passes. This tool derives the exam from the **textbook pages only** (anti-circularity) and runs a closed-book simulated student twice — once with only prior-grade knowledge (pre-test control), once after "studying" only the packet — then grades the delta per learning objective. Outcomes per objective: `already_known` / `learned` / `not_taught` (teaching-equivalence failure — `mentioned`-but-never-explained counts as not taught) / `not_learnable` (taught but the closed-book student still failed). Engagement/motivation is explicitly out of scope (user decision: "their responsibility; we fulfill ours"). Normal audit = 5 logical `agent.run_phase(schema=…, transport=api)` calls (exam → pre-test → post-test → grade → coverage); `--sensitivity` = the 7-call paired instrument-validation experiment.

**Five load-bearing methodology guarantees (each earned through 3 external GK2 gate rounds before any code):** (1) **anti-circularity** — `build_exam_prompt` receives textbook text only, never the packet. (2) **student sees the deliverable only** — `filter_deliverable` keeps `status=="done"` AND `phase_name!="extract"` AND non-empty, byte-for-byte matching the real download/Notion exports (`jobs._phase_zip`, `notion_archive`); the internal textbook-derived `extract` row must never reach the simulated student or the post-test would pass on knowledge the packet never delivered. (3) **immutable paired baseline** — `paired_audit` derives the exam once, sits the pre-test once, and grades ALL sittings in ONE combined call, so the pre baseline is byte-identical across the two legs (the examiner cannot grade the same pre answers two ways and drift an objective between `already_known`/`learned`). (4) **blinded grader** — `_grade_blinded` relabels sittings to opaque `s0/s1/…` in the grading prompt and remaps to semantics only after the verdict returns, so the examiner can't favor the real packet or mark down the control. (5) **dual-gate sensitivity** — `PairedResult.sensitivity_pass` requires BOTH `control.learned_count == 0` (student path — the empty control must teach NOTHING; any learned objective is post-test leakage, tightened from the initial `< normal` at gate-4) AND every control-leg coverage row `absent` (coverage path — an empty document scored `taught` is a broken scorer); `sensitivity_failures()` reports the two paths distinctly. The gate validates the *instrument*, not the real packet's quality (which `normal.teaching_equivalent`/`.learnable` report separately). The negative control is a TRUE empty-packet sentinel (`CONTROL_STUDY_MD`), not phase-ablation — verified that the residual phases (`memory-check`, `practice-error-detection`, boss-arena) carry explicit answer keys, so a gutted-but-nonempty packet could still teach and equal learned-counts would not prove the instrument broken. Fail-loud throughout: any dead/unparsed/protocol-inconsistent scorer raises `TeachingAuditError` (no degrade-to-pass, no silent defaults); `validate_protocol` always precedes `aggregate` so `all([])` can never fabricate a clean verdict; blank objective/question/answer-key content is rejected.

**Gate history:** plan gated by the user's external reviewer (GK2) across **three rounds** before approval — round 1 (5 blockers: extract-leak, uncontrolled gutted comparison, malformed-scorer clean verdict, evidence discarded, `mentioned` mislabeled), round 2 (2 blockers: non-paired baseline → combined single grade call; invalid phase-ablation control → empty control), round 3 (2 blockers: grader not blinded → opaque labels; coverage-path escape → dual gate). Each round's blockers were verified against real code before folding in; every one was a legitimate methodology hole, not style. A round-3 re-paste against a stale checkout was caught (cited line numbers matched the pre-fix commit; branch was unpushed) and the already-committed fixes were shown rather than re-done.

**Execution (subagent-driven, 5 code tasks, controller stress-tested every commit):** Task 1 schemas+validation+aggregate (`76cf311`), Task 2 prompt builders (`fec32b3`), Task 3 loaders (`5b7d1b2`), Task 4 orchestrator — blinded grader + dual gate (`8c9ea46`), Task 5 report+api-only CLI (`aebb0a2`). Final independent whole-branch review (Opus): **APPROVED, 0 Critical / 0 Important / 5 Minor** (non-blocking), all five methodology guarantees verified in code.

**Live acceptance (Task 6, real api, bounded per money rule):** one paired run against production `edu_copy`, job `1070faec` (g9 biology uz, "Oqsillar. Aminokislotalar", pp63–65). Result **`sensitivity: PASS`** — real packet learned=3/3 (pre 0/2 → post 1.5–2/2, coverage taught/taught/mentioned), empty control learned=0/3 (pre 0/2 → post 0/2, all coverage `absent` → `not_taught`); both gates held. Pre-test 0/2 across all objectives confirmed the exam is genuinely lesson-specific (no latent-knowledge leak — the design's key risk). **Cost $0.1980** across 7 logical calls (examiner gemini-2.5-pro, student gemini-2.5-flash). Attribution verified: exactly 7 `agent_usages` rows (`teach:exam`×1, `teach:pretest`×1, `teach:posttest`×2, `teach:grade`×1, `teach:coverage`×2), all `auth_mode='api'`, all `homework_job_id` NULL, zero retries. No prompt calibration needed.

**Scope/limits:** standalone/offline, read-only except the `agent_usages` ledger rows every `run_phase` writes (no domain/pipeline/schema changes, no migration). One job per invocation (batch sweeps out of scope — money rule). The JSON report retains the full evidence chain (exam+keys, both sittings, per-question grades w/ evidence, coverage w/ evidence) so a human can audit a false-positive `learned`. Honest limitation, documented: it measures teaching *under simulation* — the simulated student reads more charitably than a real 8th-grader, so it catches structural learnability failures (missing examples, broken sequencing, unexplained terms) but not register/readability; classroom data remains the only ground truth. CQ-E integration (consume as an advisory dimension) and real-student validation left as follow-ups.

**Post-PR gate (GK2 on the code PR #102, 4 findings folded in-branch before merge):** (1) sensitivity student-path tightened from `control < normal` to `control.learned_count == 0` — the old rule passed a leaking control when the real packet learned more, and failed a genuinely-ineffective packet (0/0) as a false instrument-failure; normal-packet quality is now judged strictly separately. (2) the JSON report gained the **primary evidence** it was missing — `book_id`/`toc_entry_id`/`page_start`/`page_end` identity plus immutable `textbook_text` + `study_md` snapshots, each with a sha256, so a human can re-verify exam↔textbook and coverage↔packet after the source or outputs change. (3) the examiner's declared **3-6 objective** bound is now enforced fail-loud (`_validate_objective_count`, wired at the examiner-output boundary before any student/grader call) — a 1-objective response omitting most of a lesson previously produced a clean verdict; unit-tested at counts 1/3/6/7 plus a wired-flow test. (4) worklog renumbered 0147→0148 (PR #101 owns 0147). Module suite 28→**32 passed**.

**Verified:** module suite **32 passed**; full backend suite green; CLI `--help` api-only (no `--transport`). Live paired smoke PASS at $0.1980 (re-verified after the gate-4 fixes — control learned 0, all control coverage absent, evidence block populated). Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/shipped/2026-07-17-teaching-equivalence-audit.md`.

## 0151 — 2026-07-18 — Nggaev-v2 (direct) — Dashboard: language tabs are now true per-language views

**What:** User rejected the 0150 semantics ("what's the point of language toggles if I don't see only the selected language's subjects?") — the tabs scoped homework OUTPUT language but still listed every book, so the Русский tab was a wall of `[UZ] 0 of N Ready to start` rows. New rule (pure `visibleForLang`, TDD RED-first): a book renders on a tab only if it has something IN that language — its textbook IS that language, OR homework in that language exists/is in flight (entry counts are already tab-scoped by the endpoint). Everything else falls into the collapsed gap bucket, reworded per-language ("Nothing in Russian yet (N)" — a uz textbook may exist, so "No textbook yet" would lie); grade-card header + summary tile + empty-state wording made language-aware too. Result on live data: ru tab 57 rows → 5 (4 ru textbooks + the uz G9 algebra with 1 ru packet, chip `[UZ]` explaining the source).

**Files:** `web/src/lib/subject-coverage.ts`+`.test.ts` (visibleForLang + 6 assertions), `web/src/routes/dashboard.tsx` (filter before grouping), `web/src/components/dashboard/grade-card.tsx`, `coverage-summary.tsx`.

**Verified:** npm test 12/12, tsc clean, build clean; ru-tab preview against the live endpoint = exactly the 5 expected rows. FE-only, no migration; dist rebuilt on the serving host (this Mac = 192.168.1.16 — NOTE: the old memory pinning it at .2 is stale DHCP).

Traced end-to-end: routing was CORRECT (each bar links its own book_id; all 57 served entries verified against independently recomputed truth — fresh SQL + classifier, zero mismatches incl. batch/paused fields).
