## 0149 — 2026-07-18 — feat/coverage-dashboard — Subject coverage dashboard (grade-first, plain language, read-only)

**What:** A standalone `/dashboard` page answering, for a NON-TECHNICAL reader, "how is homework generation going for each grade's subjects?" — deliberately separate from `/monitor` (which stays batch-centric and is untouched). Pick a grade → its subjects render as readable rows with a progress bar, a plain-English status, and a stuck-lesson note; subjects with no textbook collapse into a `No textbook yet (N)` disclosure. Language tabs (uz/en/ru), a 4-tile overview, drill-in links to the book page. **Read-only by design** — no launch/resume buttons on a page aimed at non-technical viewers; the launcher already owns those flows.

**Why a new endpoint (`GET /api/v1/dashboard/coverage?output_language=`):** reuse was verified impossible. `GET /books` returns no TOC, no counts, no `toc_ready_at`, and caps at 100; `GET /jobs/batches` only lists *launched* books (so "ready, not started" — arguably the most useful cell — is invisible) and is already 3N+1 via `batches.list_with_rollups`. Client-side assembly would cost one `GET /books/{id}` per book. The new route is four set-based queries (books, TOC rows in bulk, latest-job-per-(book,toc_entry), newest batch per book) plus one pure `classify_entries` pass per book.

**The denominator is lesson-class TOC rows, never `toc_total`.** `toc_total_for_batch` counts headers/tests/revision/answer-key rows, so a naive denominator would tell a reader "12 of 40" for a book with 28 real lessons. There is no SQL path — the classifier is pure Python and must run per book.

**Gate finding (folded before execution, and the single most important behaviour here):** the job tally must be scoped to the SAME lesson rows as the denominator. Pre-#89 launches were unfiltered, so legacy books carry real `done` jobs on test/revision rows; tallying those against a lesson-only denominator lets non-lesson work mask a failed lesson and report "Finished". The repo therefore returns `{book_id: {toc_entry_id: latest_status}}` (still one query) and `subject_coverage._lesson_tally` classifies once and scopes both numbers to the same row set (folding `cancelling`→`running`). **Verified against real data:** `geometriya-g7-11` g9 carries 3 done jobs on non-lesson rows — the endpoint reports `done=45` matching `lesson_done=45`, not 48; pre-fix that book would have read `48 ≥ 45` → "Finished" while incomplete. Also RED-proved in unit test: removing the scoping guard yields `done=4` vs the asserted `2`.

**Architecture:** pure logic split out and unit-tested on both sides — `app/services/subject_coverage.py` (`build_coverage`, no DB/IO) and `web/src/lib/subject-coverage.ts` (`coverageState` 10-state machine + `groupByGrade` + `summarizeGrade`, no React), following the `batch-status.ts` house pattern. The route, repo, schemas and three presentational components are thin around them. The FE `0/0 must never read as Finished` guard is also RED-proved (removing it fails the test).

**Design decision — gaps shown but collapsed:** the subject registry has **no per-grade curriculum map** (grade lives only on `Book.grade`), so expanding all 26 subjects per grade would assert nonsense (Geometry in Grade 1). Subjects with a textbook render first sorted by urgency (`STATE_ORDER`: needs_attention → … → finished), the rest collapse into the gap disclosure. Filed `per-grade-curriculum-map-1` on WISHLIST rather than inventing curriculum knowledge.

**Acceptance ($0 — no model calls, read-only against production):** endpoint HTTP 200, 57 entries across grades 5–11; lesson-scoping cross-checked on 5 books, all matching. Render verified in a real browser against a temporary worktree server on :8010 (the user's :8000 untouched, `WORKER_CONCURRENCY=0` so the throwaway process could not claim live jobs; killed afterwards): nav entry, urgency sorting, plain-language states, the multi-textbook case (History g9 showing two editions), collapsed gap list, drill-in links, and Geometry g9 correctly reading 45 of 45. The live render surfaced one real copy defect — "1 lesson need a look" — fixed to "needs" (subject-verb agreement matters on a page whose whole purpose is reading naturally).

**Honest limitation:** the two real-DB api integration tests are correct-by-inspection and skip-by-default, but were **not executed** — the app's DB user lacks `CREATE DATABASE` so no scratch DB was possible, and seeding fake book rows into the production campaign DB was declined (a killed process would strand a fake book visible in Library and this very dashboard). The API-layer lesson-scoping proof rides on the read-only production acceptance above, which exercised exactly that path.

**Verified:** backend suite **1830 passed / 292 skipped**; FE `npm run test` 12/12 lib suites, `tsc --noEmit` clean, `npm run build` clean. `/monitor` and `components/fleet/*` provably unmodified across the whole branch. No migration, no writes, no mutations. Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/shipped/2026-07-17-coverage-dashboard.md`.

**What:** `uv run uvicorn viewer_main:app --host 0.0.0.0 --port 8001` — a second FastAPI process serving ONLY the coverage dashboard, for handing to non-technical viewers. Structural read-only: the process contains no worker, no lifespan sweeps, and exactly two API routes (static `/health` `{"ok":true}` + `GET /api/v1/dashboard/coverage`), proven by a route-enumeration test that also asserts no route accepts a mutating method; docs/redoc/openapi disabled; `/redoc` reserved in the SPA fallback (caught live: once `dist-viewer` existed the catch-all served the shell there). Auth = new `DASHBOARD_TOKEN` (comma-separated) via `get_viewer_user` — **header-only** Bearer (no `?token=`), validates ONLY viewer tokens; startup REFUSES on empty token AND on `dashboard∩operator` overlap. FE = same codebase, second build: `npm run build:viewer` (`--config vite.viewer.config.ts` — NOT `--mode`, which loads the default config), `IS_VIEWER` flag trims the router to login+dashboard, no nav, `BookLine` renders a plain div (viewer has no /book pages); `layout.tsx`'s `wide` check also branches (else operator path literals leak into the bundle). Bundle purity gated: `/monitor` + `/book/` markers 6/15 in `dist`, 0/0 in `dist-viewer`.

**Gate rounds:** plan gated twice by the external reviewer (round 2 = 5 corrections: no-link rows, token-overlap refusal, header-only auth, `--host 0.0.0.0` + `--config` fixes, surface enforcement incl. static health + route enumeration) — all folded pre-code and materially enforced in tests. T2 review Low fixed in-branch (dead `__main__` referencing nonexistent `settings.host/port` — note: `main.py:201` carries the same latent bug, filed nowhere yet, harmless via documented run path).

**Live acceptance ($0):** on :8001 against prod DB — viewer token 200 (57 entries), operator token 401, `?token=` 401, viewer token on :8000 401 (separation both ways), `/api/v1/books` 404, docs/redoc/openapi 404, SPA shell served, empty-token and overlap-token startups refuse loudly. Suite 1846/293 (+15 viewer tests); FE 12/12 + tsc + both builds.

## 0152 — 2026-07-18 — feat/teach-audit-offset — Teaching-audit page-offset guard (widened window + title-anchored examiner)

**What:** Closes `teaching-audit-page-offset-1`. The teaching audit sliced textbook pages with `agent.read_page_range_text(pdf, toc.page_start, toc.page_end)` — but `toc_entries.page_start/page_end` hold the book's **PRINTED** page numbers while that helper takes **PHYSICAL** PDF pages. On books where front matter makes those disagree, the audit derived its exam from a **neighbouring lesson** and issued a confident verdict about the wrong lesson. Found in the field: a G9 history packet (`a92e62ae`, job `e9af64ee`) scored a false ❌ NO/NO — the exam was about §28 *IJTIMOIY-IQTISODIY HAYOT* while the packet correctly covered *Qo'qon xonligida davlat boshqaruvi*. **Generation is immune** (extract reads whole-book text and locates lessons by title); only page-range slicers inherit the trap.

**Deterministic repair was measured and REJECTED (this is why the shipped fix looks different from the filed wishlist item).** Probing the specimen's 35 TOC entries read-only: only **19 of 35 section titles are findable in extracted page text at all** — headings are styled, split across lines, or image-only — so the filed "verify the sliced pages contain the section title, else fail loud" check would have cried *page offset suspected* on **~46% of correct slices**, blocking far more verdicts than it saved. And the implied offsets **scatter** (`-3×7, -2×5, -1×1, +1×5, +2×1` — modal `-3` at just **37% agreement**), so a single per-book offset correction would mis-slice too. Both deterministic approaches fail on real data; the probe numbers are recorded in the WISHLIST entry so nobody re-proposes them.

**Shipped instead (3 parts):** (1) `_PAGE_WINDOW_MARGIN = 4` — the loader reads the recorded range with `margin=4`, covering the observed `-3..+2` spread with slack; (2) **the examiner is anchored to the lesson title** — `build_exam_prompt` now tells it the window is deliberately wider and may include neighbours, instructs it to locate the lesson titled *X* and derive **ONLY** from it, and to return **ZERO objectives** if that lesson is absent rather than substituting another; (3) the zero-objective path in `_validate_exam` fails loud naming the suspected page offset. A model reading the page recognizes a heading that string matching cannot (that is exactly the 16/35 gap), so offset books now produce a **correct verdict instead of being skipped** — correct-or-loud, never confidently wrong. Methodology guarantees (blinded grader, immutable paired baseline, deliverable-only filter, empty-packet control) are untouched — the margin widens only what the *examiner* sees; the student/grader flow is unchanged.

**Acceptance (real api, $0.3414 total, both legs single-audit 5 calls):** (a) **G9 offset specimen** `e9af64ee` — the exam now derives from the right lesson, objectives unmistakably state-governance (`mingboshi`, `beklarbegi`, `devonbegi`, `mehtar`, `shig'ovul`, `qozikalon`, `oqsoqollik`); $0.1986. The verdict is still NO/NO but now for **real, specific reasons** on the correct lesson (O4 ethnic composition `mentioned`→`not_taught`; O3 local governance `taught` but 0.5/2 →`not_learnable`), not because it examined a different lesson. (b) **G6 clean-baseline regression** `1a129bd6` — $0.1428; teaching-equivalence held YES with all 3 objectives `taught`, but `learnable` flipped to NO on O1 (1/2 against the 1.5/2 threshold) versus the operator's earlier clean run. **Investigated rather than assumed:** a read-only window probe confirmed all three objectives' content (Gomer/Iliada/Troya, teatr/Sofokl/Aristofan, Fidiy/Dedal/ustun) is present in the **core** pages 105-108, so the widened window did **not** leak neighbouring content into the objectives — the difference is examiner nondeterminism on a borderline objective (the exam is regenerated per run).

**Known property surfaced by the acceptance (worth remembering for sweeps):** single-run verdicts are noisy at the margins — objectives scoring 1/2 or 1.5/2 sit right on the 75% `learned` threshold and can flip between runs of the same packet. Aggregate patterns across a sweep are trustworthy; an individual borderline `not_learnable` is not, on its own, a finding. The paired `--sensitivity` mode exists for instrument validation, not for de-noising per-objective verdicts.

**Verified:** module tests **34 passed** (32 + 2 new); full backend suite **1832 passed / 292 skipped**. The zero-objective guard was RED-proved (the test failed with "Regex pattern did not match" before the change) — inherited guards are not trusted here. No migration, no writes.

## 0155 — 2026-07-20 — feat/queue-transient-retry — Queue correctness: saturation parks, transients retry, cancel always wins

**What:** Closes the systemic queue-correctness defect verified 2026-07-20 (six confirmed claims): the 600s per-attempt timer swallowed up to 5×120s fleet credential-slot waits; `str(asyncio.TimeoutError())==""` wrote blank `"<phase>: "` errors; and every phase failure was swallowed inside the pipeline (head loop, content sentinel, top-level catch), so the worker's bounded `mark_failed_with_retry` was unreachable and cancelled sibling phases leaked `running` rows. Shipped (8 tasks, TDD each): typed signals in import-free `errors.py` (`SlotSaturation`, `TransientPhaseError`, `PhaseAttemptTimeout`, `is_slot_saturation` over the limiter's marker string); `_spawn` returns the slot-exhaustion 429 after ONE attempt; `_run_with_failover` raises the typed timeout and `SlotSaturation` (marker checked before session-limit/classify); `_execute_one_phase` splits transient (propagate → worker bounded retry, `queue_max_attempts=3`) from hard (DB-first terminal mark, best-effort event publish via `_publish_error_event`) with a marker fallback catching the vision-extract bypass; saturation pass-throughs at judge, solver, and both regen catches; scheduler resets abandoned sibling rows (`reset_abandoned_phases` — `pending` on requeue/park, `failed` on hard/cancel) via shielded `_abandon_inflight`; worker parks `SlotSaturation` (`requeue_slot_saturated`: attempt refunded, `scheduled_at += slot_saturation_requeue_seconds` (90s), guarded `status='running'`); cancel-wins everywhere (`_finalize_if_cancelling`: fresh column-scalar read — never `session.get`, the BE-02 stale-identity-map lesson — finalizing via `mark_cancelled`), also retrofitted onto `mark_failed_with_retry`.

**Gate rounds:** plan gated three times pre-code (round 2: five corrections incl. bypass paths, event ordering, real e2e, pending-vs-failed rows, cancel guards, name fixes; round 3: claim off-by-one, stale-identity finalize + `mark_cancelled` phase rows, placeholder removal) — all folded and materially enforced in tests. Mid-execution the controller caught a Task-2 regression (stale limiter-wiring test encoding the removed retry contract) via bisection and had it realigned (63acbd5).

**Verified:** full suite **1881 passed / 302 skipped** (canonical, no flag); real-DB legs green on scratch `edu_scratch_qc` incl. the two-session stale-identity-map race and the REAL e2e chain (`Worker._execute_job` → `pipeline.run` → `_run_with_failover` hung-provider → delayed-pending with attempt burned → terminal-failed on last attempt; only the provider call, clock, and event bus stubbed). Acceptance smoke: one real gemini-2.5-flash api call through the full production path (limiter slot + Vertex SDK) — reply `OK`, 207in/23out, ≈$0.0001. Final whole-branch review (broad pass): READY TO MERGE, findings all Minor → filed as `session-limit-requeue-cancel-race-1`, `parked-job-phase-row-cosmetics-1`, `reset-abandoned-status-assert-1`. No migration.

## 0156 — 2026-07-21 — fix/orphan-phase-reconciliation — Queue sweeps reconcile phase rows (marker-aware, evidence-preserving)

**What:** Closes `orphan-phase-reconciliation-1` (GK round-5 scope reminder on #109; field case: 10 `done` + 1 orphaned `running` phase → parent reclaimed `pending` → `fail_exhausted_pending_jobs` terminal-failed the job with ZERO failed phase rows — invisible to failed/cancelled-based watchers). `reset_abandoned_phases` widened into the batch reconciliation primitive (`job_ids: Sequence` with empty no-op, `phase_names=None`=all/`[]`=no-op, explicit `source_statuses`, `include_orphan_failed`), constant `ORPHANED_RESTART_MESSAGE` single-sourced (main.py's two boot-sweep literals now consume it — the boot sweep pre-marks pending/running rows failed BEFORE the startup reclaim, so pending/running-only filters match nothing at boot; verified main.py:41-51). Both sweeps reconcile via `UPDATE … RETURNING id` in the same transaction: reclaim → `running` + orphan-marker rows → `pending` (error cleared); exhausted → `pending`/`running` + orphan-marker rows → `failed` with the sweep message. `done` rows and genuinely-failed rows (any other error, incl. NULL) are never touched — SQL-compile-verified predicate parenthesization at review.

**Verified:** suite **1881 passed / 311 skipped**; real-DB 10/10 on scratch `edu_scratch_qc` incl. the five spec tests (field-case pin; reclaim-then-exhausted same-transaction chain in main.py order; startup-marker path with genuine-failure preservation). Mid-execution fallout handled: `test_fail_exhausted_pending.py`'s fake session only modeled `.rowcount` — realigned to the RETURNING contract and STRENGTHENED (now asserts the reconciliation call args). $0 — no spawn-path code, no model calls, no migration.
